\begin{remark}[Status and Gaps]
The framework presented here is complete at the schematic level, but several core analytic inputs remain to be proved (or imported from the literature in a form compatible with the nonlinear iteration). In particular:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Uniform coercivity in the ergoregion}: A fully gauge-invariant coercive energy for gravitational perturbations that remains positive for all $\chi<1$.
    \item \textbf{Uniform Morawetz / trapping control}: Integrated local energy decay estimates that treat the full trapped set and remain quantitative as $\chi\to 1$.
    \item \textbf{Superradiance control without derivative loss}: A robust mechanism (frequency-localized currents / microlocal estimates) compatible with nonlinear commutations.
    \item \textbf{Gauge and modulation}: Global propagation of the chosen gauge (e.g. generalized harmonic) and a modulation scheme to track the drift in Kerr parameters $(M_f,a_f)$.
    \item \textbf{Near-extremal regime}: A precise interface between near-horizon dynamics and the exterior estimates (where constants degenerate as $\chi\to 1$).
\end{enumerate}
Each item is a well-posed technical problem, but proving all of them in a single framework is the remaining barrier to a complete proof.
\end{remark}

%==============================================================================
\section{Toward Teukolsky-Starobinsky Coercivity}
%==============================================================================

We record a target coercivity statement and outline an approach based on the Teukolsky-Starobinsky identities.

\begin{assumption}[Teukolsky-Starobinsky Coercivity (Target Estimate)]\label{thm:TS-rigorous}
For Kerr spacetime with $|a| < M$, the Teukolsky-Starobinsky identity provides a coercive energy functional. Specifically, there exist constants $c_1, c_2 > 0$ depending only on $a/M$ such that:
\begin{equation}
c_1 \left(\|\psi_0\|^2_{H^1} + \|\psi_4\|^2_{H^1}\right) \leq E_{TS}[\psi] \leq c_2 \left(\|\psi_0\|^2_{H^1} + \|\psi_4\|^2_{H^1}\right)
\end{equation}
where $\psi_0, \psi_4$ are the extreme Weyl scalars and $E_{TS}$ is the Teukolsky-Starobinsky energy.
\end{assumption}

\begin{proof}[Proof strategy (not a completed proof)]
We outline a four-step route to establishing the estimate.

\textbf{Step 1: The Starobinsky-Teukolsky constant.}

The Teukolsky equation for spin-weight $s = \pm 2$ perturbations separates in Boyer-Lindquist coordinates. For a mode $\psi = e^{-i\omega t + im\phi} R(r) S(\theta)$, the radial equation is:
\begin{equation}
\Delta^{-s}\frac{d}{dr}\left(\Delta^{s+1}\frac{dR}{dr}\right) + \left(\frac{K^2 - 2is(r-M)K}{\Delta} + 4is\omega r - \lambda\right)R = 0
\end{equation}
where $K = (r^2 + a^2)\omega - am$ and $\lambda$ is the angular separation constant.

The Starobinsky-Teukolsky identity relates $\psi_0$ and $\psi_4$ via a fourth-order differential operator $\mathcal{D}^4$:
\begin{equation}
\psi_4 = C_{TS}(\omega, a, \ell, m) \cdot \mathcal{D}^4[\psi_0]
\end{equation}

The Starobinsky constant is:
\begin{equation}
|C_{TS}|^2 = (\lambda + 2)^2[(\lambda + 2)^2 + 4a\omega(m - a\omega)][(\lambda + 2)^2 + 36a\omega(m - a\omega)] + (2a\omega)^2(40a^2\omega^2 - (\lambda + 2)^2)^2 + 144M^2\omega^2
\end{equation}

\textbf{Step 2: Lower bound on $|C_{TS}|^2$.}

\begin{assumption}[Lower Bound Input for $C_{TS}$]\label{lem:CTS-lower}
For all $|a| < M$, all $\omega \in \mathbb{C}$ with $\text{Re}(\omega) \neq 0$, and all $\ell \geq 2$:
\begin{equation}
|C_{TS}|^2 \geq c_\ell^2 \cdot \max\{1, (M|\omega|)^4\}
\end{equation}
where $c_\ell = (\ell - 1)\ell(\ell + 1)(\ell + 2)/12 > 0$.
\end{assumption}

\begin{proof}[Heuristic proof strategy]
We analyze three regimes:

\textit{Case 1: Low frequency $|M\omega| \leq 1$.}
The angular eigenvalue satisfies $\lambda \geq \ell(\ell+1) - 2 - |a\omega|^2$ for $|a\omega| \leq 1$. Thus:
\begin{equation}
(\lambda + 2)^2 \geq (\ell(\ell+1) - |a\omega|^2)^2 \geq \left(\ell(\ell+1) - 1\right)^2 = (\ell^2 + \ell - 1)^2
\end{equation}
For $\ell \geq 2$, this gives $|C_{TS}|^2 \geq (\ell^2 + \ell - 1)^4 \geq 81$ for $\ell = 2$.

\textit{Case 2: High frequency $|M\omega| > 1$.}
The $144M^2\omega^2$ term dominates:
\begin{equation}
|C_{TS}|^2 \geq 144M^2|\omega|^2 \geq 144 M^2 \omega^2
\end{equation}

\textit{Case 3: Superradiant regime $0 < \omega < m\Omega_H$.}
Here $m - a\omega > 0$. The key observation is that even when $a\omega(m - a\omega)$ contributes negatively to some terms, the structure of $|C_{TS}|^2$ as a sum of squares ensures positivity:
\begin{equation}
|C_{TS}|^2 = |A + iB|^2 = A^2 + B^2 \geq 0
\end{equation}
with equality only when $A = B = 0$, which requires $\omega = \lambda = 0$ (impossible for $\ell \geq 2$).

Combining all cases:
\begin{equation}
|C_{TS}|^2 \geq c_\ell^2 \cdot \max\{1, (M|\omega|)^4\}
\end{equation}
where $c_\ell = \min\{(\ell^2 + \ell - 1)^2, 12\} / \max\{1, M^2\}$ can be computed explicitly.
\end{proof}

\textbf{Step 3: Construction of the coercive energy.}

Define the Teukolsky-Starobinsky energy:
\begin{equation}
E_{TS}[\psi] = \int_{\Sigma_t} \left(|\mathcal{D}^2\psi_0|^2 + \frac{1}{|C_{TS}|^2}|\psi_4|^2 + \text{cross terms}\right) r^2 \sin\theta \, dr\, d\theta\, d\phi
\end{equation}

The integration measure is $d\mu = r^2 \sin\theta\, dr\, d\theta\, d\phi$ on constant-$t$ hypersurfaces $\Sigma_t$.

Using Assumption \ref{lem:CTS-lower}:
\begin{equation}
E_{TS} \geq \frac{1}{c_2^2}\int_{\Sigma_t} \left(|\psi_4|^2 + |\mathcal{D}^2\psi_0|^2\right) d\mu
\end{equation}

\textbf{Step 4: Equivalence with Sobolev norms.}

The operator $\mathcal{D}^2$ is elliptic away from the horizon. Standard elliptic regularity gives:
\begin{equation}
\|\mathcal{D}^2\psi_0\|_{L^2} \sim \|\psi_0\|_{H^2}
\end{equation}

Near the horizon, the red-shift effect provides additional positivity. Combining:
\begin{equation}
c_1 \left(\|\psi_0\|^2_{H^1} + \|\psi_4\|^2_{H^1}\right) \leq E_{TS}[\psi] \leq c_2 \left(\|\psi_0\|^2_{H^1} + \|\psi_4\|^2_{H^1}\right)
\end{equation}

The constants are:
\begin{align}
c_1 &= \frac{(1 - \chi^2)^{1/2}}{C(\ell)} \quad \text{(degenerates at extremality)} \\
c_2 &= C(\ell) \cdot (1 + \chi^2)
\end{align}
where $C(\ell) = O(\ell^4)$ depends only on the angular momentum quantum number.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Illustrative $\ell = 2$ Scaling Model]
For gravitational perturbations ($\ell = 2$, $s = -2$), one may use a schematic model of the form
\begin{align}
c_1 &\geq \frac{\sqrt{1-\chi^2}}{150} \\
c_2 &\leq 150(1 + \chi^2)
\end{align}
\end{remark}

%==============================================================================
\section{Schematic Quantitative Consequences}
%==============================================================================

This section records only the quantitative conclusions that are actually justified by the preceding abstract framework. We do \emph{not} claim explicit numerical coefficients for decay exponents, resonance locations, or near-extremal constants.

\subsection{What Is Not Proved Quantitatively}

\begin{remark}
The manuscript does not prove formulas such as
\begin{equation}
p(\chi)=3-1.47\chi^2+0.23\chi^4+\cdots,
\qquad
\gamma(\chi)=\frac{\sqrt{1-\chi^2}}{4M}(1+\epsilon(\chi)),
\end{equation}
nor any explicit near-extremal constant of the form $c_0=1/(4\sqrt2)\pm 2\%$. Such statements would require a separate combination of sharp mode analysis, resolvent estimates with tracked constants, and in part numerical spectral input. They are therefore omitted from the rigorous core of the paper.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Abstract Coercivity-to-Gap Principle}

%==============================================================================
\section{The Centerpiece Theorem: Conditional Coercivity Implies a Spectral Gap}
%==============================================================================

The correct mathematical takeaway is abstract rather than numeric: if one can place the linearized Kerr dynamics in an operator framework where a coercive Teukolsky-Starobinsky energy yields resolvent control, then one obtains a resonance-free strip and hence a spectral gap. The missing work is not the logic of this implication, but the Kerr-specific operator theory needed to implement it with sharp constants.

\subsection{Statement of the Main Theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional Coercivity-Spectral Gap Correspondence]\label{thm:coercivity-spectral-main}\label{thm:coercivity-spectral-gap}
Fix a subextremal Kerr exterior with $\chi = |a|/M < 1$. Assume there exists a Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}_\chi$, a closed generator $\mathcal{G}_\chi$ for the linearized evolution, and a quadratic form $E_{TS}$ on $\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{G}_\chi)$ such that:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item $E_{TS}$ is coercive:
    \begin{equation}
    E_{TS}[u] \ge c_{TS}(\chi)\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_\chi}^2
    \end{equation}
    for some $c_{TS}(\chi)>0$;
    \item the resolvent $R_\chi(\omega)=(\mathcal{G}_\chi-\omega)^{-1}$ is meromorphic in the lower half-plane and its poles coincide with the quasinormal frequencies;
    \item the coercive estimate can be converted into a resolvent bound of the form
    \begin{equation}
    \|R_\chi(\omega)\|_{\mathcal{H}_\chi\to\mathcal{H}_\chi}
    \le \frac{C_{\mathrm{res}}(\chi)}{|\operatorname{Im}\omega|}
    \qquad \text{for } \operatorname{Im}\omega < -A(\chi)c_{TS}(\chi),
    \end{equation}
    for some $A(\chi),C_{\mathrm{res}}(\chi)>0$.
\end{enumerate}
Then the quasinormal spectrum of $\mathcal{G}_\chi$ is disjoint from the strip
\begin{equation}
\{-A(\chi)c_{TS}(\chi) < \operatorname{Im}\omega < 0\},
\end{equation}
and therefore
\begin{equation}
\gamma(\chi) \ge A(\chi)c_{TS}(\chi).
\end{equation}
In particular, any explicit lower bound for $c_{TS}(\chi)$ immediately yields a corresponding lower bound for the spectral gap, up to the operator-theoretic constant $A(\chi)$.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Proof of the Main Theorem}

The proof is an abstract spectral-theoretic implication once the Kerr-specific assumptions above are available.

\begin{proof}
Assumption (3) already supplies a uniform resolvent bound in the lower half-plane below the line $\operatorname{Im}\omega=-A(\chi)c_{TS}(\chi)$. Since poles of the resolvent coincide with quasinormal frequencies by assumption (2), no such pole can lie in the strip
\begin{equation}
-A(\chi)c_{TS}(\chi) < \operatorname{Im}\omega < 0,
\end{equation}
because the norm of the resolvent would then blow up near the pole, contradicting the assumed uniform estimate. This yields the stated resonance-free strip and therefore the lower bound $\gamma(\chi)\ge A(\chi)c_{TS}(\chi)$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Step 4: Heuristic comparison with QNM asymptotics}

The preceding coercivity-to-gap discussion should not be read as determining the sharp spectral gap or an optimal universal constant. In the geometric-optics regime one expects a relation of the schematic form
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{Im}(\omega_{n\ell m}) \sim -\Bigl(n+\tfrac12\Bigr)\lambda_{\mathrm{Lyapunov}}
\end{equation}
for highly oscillatory modes associated with unstable trapped null geodesics. This heuristic is useful for interpretation, but by itself it does not control low angular momentum modes, modes near the superradiant threshold, or the sharp constant in any global decay estimate. We therefore stop short of asserting any universal QNM inequality beyond the conditional spectral-gap statements already isolated above.

%==============================================================================
\section{The Remaining Trapped-Superradiant Gap}
\label{sec:trapped-superradiant}
%==============================================================================

The reduction results of Section~\ref{sec:nonlinear-framework} identify the remaining obstruction very concretely. After the separated-mode flux decomposition, the projected ergoregion estimate, and the microlocal positivity criterion, the unresolved issue is no longer a diffuse ``missing proof'' but a specific positivity problem for the hidden-symmetry symbol near the superradiant part of the trapped set.

\begin{assumption}[Periodic-orbit average positivity]\label{ass:periodic-average-positivity}
There exists $c_{\mathrm{po}}>0$ such that for every periodic null bicharacteristic $\gamma \subset \Gamma \cap \Sigma_{\mathrm{sr}}$ with period $T_\gamma$ one has
\begin{equation}
\frac{1}{T_\gamma}\int_0^{T_\gamma} a_{KY}(\exp(tH_p)\rho)\,dt
\ge
c_{\mathrm{po}}\frac{1}{T_\gamma}\int_0^{T_\gamma} q_{\mathrm{sr}}(\exp(tH_p)\rho)\,dt
\end{equation}
for $\rho \in \gamma$.
\end{assumption}

\begin{assumption}[Microlocal globalization near $\Gamma \cap \Sigma_{\mathrm{sr}}$]\label{ass:microlocal-globalization}
The local escape functions produced away from recurrent trajectories can be patched, using the normally hyperbolic structure of the trapped set and standard pseudodifferential remainder control, into a global symbol $g_{\mathrm{sr}}$ defined in a neighborhood of $\Gamma \cap \Sigma_{\mathrm{sr}}$ such that
\begin{equation}
a_{KY} + \frac12 H_p g_{\mathrm{sr}} \ge c_{\mathrm{gl}} q_{\mathrm{sr}}
\end{equation}
there for some $c_{\mathrm{gl}}>0$.
\end{assumption}

\begin{corollary}[Conditional closure of the superradiant branch]\label{cor:conditional-superradiant-closure}
Assume \ref{ass:periodic-average-positivity} and \ref{ass:microlocal-globalization}. Then the projected superradiant estimate required in Proposition~\ref{prop:integrated-ergosphere} holds. Consequently the regional coercivity reduction of Proposition~\ref{prop:regional-coercivity} applies, and the abstract continuity lemma closes the nonlinear bootstrap provided the remaining nonsuperradiant Morawetz and gauge estimates are available.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:flowbox-transport}, away from the trapped superradiant core the transport equation for $g$ is locally solvable. Proposition~\ref{prop:periodic-orbit-criterion} shows that recurrent bicharacteristics impose an orbit-average compatibility condition, and Assumption~\ref{ass:periodic-average-positivity} supplies precisely that positivity requirement. Assumption~\ref{ass:microlocal-globalization} then upgrades the local constructions to the symbol inequality required by Proposition~\ref{prop:microlocal-superradiant}. Proposition~\ref{prop:superradiant-spectral-reduction} converts this symbol inequality into the projected superradiant bound, while Proposition~\ref{prop:integrated-ergosphere} returns the physical-space ergoregion estimate. Finally Proposition~\ref{prop:regional-coercivity} and Lemma~\ref{lem:abstract-bootstrap} give the claimed closure statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[No fabricated structure is needed]
The remaining gap can therefore be stated without introducing new symbol classes or ad hoc defect terms. What is missing is a genuine microlocal theorem near $\Gamma \cap \Sigma_{\mathrm{sr}}$: an orbit-average positivity statement for $a_{KY}$ together with a globalization argument for the associated escape function. The later reduction theorem \ref{thm:periodic-to-invariant-measures} clarifies that periodic-orbit computations suffice only after one also proves a periodic-measure density property, or else verifies positivity for all invariant measures directly.
\end{remark}

%==============================================================================
\section{Open Trapped-Superradiant Problems}\label{sec:carter-resolution}
%==============================================================================

The reductions of Sections~\ref{sec:nonlinear-framework}--\ref{sec:trapped-superradiant} identify the remaining obstruction as a periodic-orbit average positivity statement for the hidden-symmetry symbol near $\Gamma \cap \Sigma_{\mathrm{sr}}$, together with a microlocal globalization step for the associated escape function. Earlier drafts of this manuscript overstated this point by presenting a completed resolution. We do \emph{not} claim such a resolution here.

\begin{remark}[What Kerr integrability does provide]
The Carter constant and complete integrability of null geodesic motion are still highly relevant. They give an explicit parametrization of trapped null geodesics and suggest that orbit averages of hidden-symmetry quantities may admit a more algebraic description than in a general stationary spacetime. What is missing is a rigorous argument turning that structure into the positivity estimate of Assumption~\ref{ass:periodic-average-positivity} with constants strong enough for the microlocal commutator argument.
\end{remark}

\begin{assumption}[Periodic-orbit positivity via Carter structure]\label{thm:carter-positivity}
One expects that the complete integrability of Kerr null geodesics can be used to verify Assumption~\ref{ass:periodic-average-positivity}; namely, that there exists $c_{\mathrm{po}}>0$ such that the hidden-symmetry symbol has a positive orbit-average lower bound on every periodic bicharacteristic in $\Gamma \cap \Sigma_{\mathrm{sr}}$.
\end{assumption}

\begin{remark}[Heuristic role of a Carter index]
It is natural to package the desired orbit-average positivity into a spin-dependent quantity, sometimes heuristically described as a ``Carter index'' for the trapped set. The manuscript does not prove the existence, exact formula, monotonicity, or extremal behavior of such an object. Any formulas of that kind should be read only as motivation for why the orbit-average problem looks tractable on Kerr.
\end{remark}

\begin{assumption}[Escape function on the superradiant trapped set]\label{thm:escape-function}
Assumption~\ref{ass:microlocal-globalization} remains an open input. In particular, one needs a smooth real symbol $g_{\mathrm{sr}}$ in a neighborhood of $\Gamma \cap \Sigma_{\mathrm{sr}}$ such that
\begin{equation}
a_{KY} + \tfrac{1}{2}H_p\, g_{\mathrm{sr}} \ge c_{\mathrm{gl}}\, q_{\mathrm{sr}}
\end{equation}
for some $c_{\mathrm{gl}}>0$.
\end{assumption}

\begin{remark}[Why this is still hard]
Even if orbit averages can be computed along individual invariant trajectories, one still needs a quantitative microlocal globalization argument with remainder control, compatible symbol classes, and robustness near recurrent bicharacteristics. That step is not supplied by Carter integrability alone.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Updated Conditional Closure}\label{subsec:closure}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional Stability After Trapped-Superradiant Closure]\label{thm:stability-strengthened}
If Assumptions~\ref{thm:carter-positivity} and~\ref{thm:escape-function} are established, then Corollary~\ref{cor:conditional-superradiant-closure} removes the trapped-superradiant obstruction from the nonlinear argument. The resulting stability theorem remains conditional on the nonsuperradiant Morawetz estimate, the gauge/modulation framework, and the coercive energy construction.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
This is an immediate restatement of Corollary~\ref{cor:conditional-superradiant-closure} together with the regional coercivity reduction of Proposition~\ref{prop:regional-coercivity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Status]
The trapped-superradiant interaction is therefore reduced to a concrete pair of open problems rather than solved in this manuscript. That reduction is useful, but it should not be confused with a proof of the required positivity or escape-function estimates.
\end{remark}

%==============================================================================
\section{The Nonsuperradiant Morawetz Input}\label{sec:morawetz-resolution}
%==============================================================================

After the trapped-superradiant branch has been isolated, a second major open input is the nonsuperradiant Morawetz estimate for gravitational perturbations on the full subextremal Kerr family. Earlier versions of the manuscript incorrectly presented this estimate as proved. We do not claim such a proof here.

\begin{remark}[Heuristic role of the Chandrasekhar transformation]
The Chandrasekhar transformation remains a plausible route to the nonsuperradiant estimate: it relates the spin-$\pm2$ Teukolsky system to Regge--Wheeler-type equations and is an established tool in linear stability analysis. However, to obtain a full-subextremal Morawetz estimate one still needs a carefully controlled transformed potential, frequency-localized boundary terms, and an inverse estimate with constants compatible with near-extremal degeneration.
\end{remark}

\begin{assumption}[Nonsuperradiant Morawetz for full subextremal Kerr]\label{thm:morawetz-full}
Let $|a| < M$ and let $\Psi_{\mathrm{ns}}$ denote the nonsuperradiant frequency projection of a linearized gravitational perturbation. One seeks an estimate of the form
\begin{equation}\label{eq:morawetz-final}
\int_0^T\!\int_{\Sigma_t}\frac{|\nabla\Psi_{\mathrm{ns}}|^2}{r^{1+\delta}}\,d^3x\,dt
\;\leq\; C(M,\delta_0)\Bigl(E[\Psi](0) + \int_0^T\!\int_{\Sigma_t}|F|^2\,d^3x\,dt\Bigr),
\end{equation}
uniformly for $\chi \le 1-\delta_0$.
\end{assumption}

\begin{remark}[What would be needed for \eqref{eq:morawetz-final}]
A proof of Assumption~\ref{thm:morawetz-full} would likely require:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item a precise transformed equation for a Chandrasekhar-type variable with controlled lower-order terms;
    \item a frequency-localized Morawetz current with correct horizon and infinity signs on the nonsuperradiant sector;
    \item an inverse transformation estimate that does not lose more than the red-shift mechanism can absorb.
\end{enumerate}
The present manuscript does not supply these ingredients in a rigorous form.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Near-extremal uniformity]\label{rem:near-extremal-morawetz}
Any such Morawetz estimate is expected to deteriorate as $\chi \to 1$, reflecting the collapse of the red-shift mechanism. The precise $\kappa$-dependence remains part of the open near-extremal problem.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Strengthened Conditional Stability]\label{thm:stability-final}
If Assumptions~\ref{thm:carter-positivity}, \ref{thm:escape-function}, and \ref{thm:morawetz-full} hold, then Theorem~\ref{thm:main-nonlinear} is reduced to the gauge/modulation framework and the final coercive-energy compatibility estimates.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Under Assumptions~\ref{thm:carter-positivity} and~\ref{thm:escape-function}, the superradiant branch closes by Corollary~\ref{cor:conditional-superradiant-closure}. Adding Assumption~\ref{thm:morawetz-full} supplies the nonsuperradiant branch. The remaining open steps are exactly the gauge/modulation and coercive-energy inputs isolated earlier in the nonlinear framework section.
\end{proof}

%==============================================================================
\section{Extremal Interface and Open Problems}
%==============================================================================

The main theorem of this paper concerns the subextremal range $|a|<M$. Extremality lies outside that theorem for structural reasons: the red-shift degenerates as $\kappa \to 0$, the near-horizon geometry changes character, and one expects Aretakis-type horizon conservation laws to replace the uniform decay mechanisms available in the strictly subextremal setting.

\begin{remark}[What the present framework does and does not say at $\chi=1$]
The analysis developed here suggests how constants in the subextremal estimates should deteriorate as $\chi \to 1$, but it does not resolve the extremal problem. In particular, this paper does not prove a nonlinear ``resolution'' of the Aretakis instability, does not derive a complete classification of extremal Kerr dynamics, and does not establish any back-reaction mechanism driving extremal data away from $\chi=1$.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Near-extremal degeneration compatible with Aretakis]
There should exist a family of subextremal energy and integrated decay estimates with constants depending explicitly on the surface gravity $\kappa$, such that after appropriate renormalization these estimates converge, as $\kappa \to 0$, to the hierarchy of horizon conservation laws and derivative growth rates discovered in the extremal problem.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Extremal open problems]
Three separate problems remain open at the extremal interface:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item identify the sharp $\kappa$-dependence of the subextremal red-shift, Morawetz, and superradiant estimates;
    \item compare the limiting subextremal energies with the Aretakis charges on the extremal horizon;
    \item understand whether any nonlinear modulation or back-reaction mechanism selects a canonical near-extremal asymptotic regime.
\end{enumerate}
These questions are important, but they are not settled here.
\end{remark}

%==============================================================================
\section{A Speculative Program Beyond the Present Framework}
%==============================================================================

This section is intentionally \emph{speculative}. It records possible new mathematical structures that, if developed rigorously, might help close the gap between the present conditional roadmap and a full proof of subextremal Kerr stability. None of the statements below is proved in this manuscript, and none should be read as part of the rigorous core.

\begin{definition}[Orbit-averaged coercive symbol]
Let $\Gamma_{\mathrm{sr}} \coloneqq \Gamma \cap \Sigma_{\mathrm{sr}}$ denote the trapped superradiant set. Given a real principal symbol $a(\rho)$ on a neighborhood of $\Gamma_{\mathrm{sr}}$, define its orbit average along the null Hamilton flow by
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{M}a(\rho) \coloneqq \liminf_{T\to +\infty} \frac{1}{T}\int_0^T a(\exp(tH_p)\rho)\,dt,
\end{equation}
whenever the limit inferior exists. We say that $a$ is \emph{orbit-averaged coercive} on $\Gamma_{\mathrm{sr}}$ if there exists $c_{\mathrm{avg}}>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{M}a(\rho) \ge c_{\mathrm{avg}}\, \mathcal{M}q_{\mathrm{sr}}(\rho)
\end{equation}
for every $\rho \in \Gamma_{\mathrm{sr}}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Dynamical compensation potential]
A real symbol $g_{\mathrm{dyn}}$ defined near $\Gamma_{\mathrm{sr}}$ is called a \emph{dynamical compensation potential} for $a$ if
\begin{equation}
a + \tfrac12 H_p g_{\mathrm{dyn}} \ge c_{\mathrm{dyn}} q_{\mathrm{sr}} - r_{\mathrm{dyn}}
\end{equation}
for some $c_{\mathrm{dyn}}>0$, where the remainder $r_{\mathrm{dyn}}$ vanishes on $\Gamma_{\mathrm{sr}}$ to strictly higher order than $q_{\mathrm{sr}}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{conjecture}[Dynamical compensation principle]
If the hidden-symmetry symbol $a_{KY}$ is orbit-averaged coercive on $\Gamma_{\mathrm{sr}}$, then there exists a dynamical compensation potential $g_{\mathrm{dyn}}$ for $a_{KY}$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}
This principle is intended as a sharpened replacement for pointwise positivity. Instead of demanding that $a_{KY}$ already dominate $q_{\mathrm{sr}}$ on the trapped superradiant set, one asks only for positivity after averaging along the true bicharacteristic dynamics, and then conjecturally upgrades that averaged positivity into a positive-commutator inequality.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform finite-time averaging on compact invariant sets]
\label{thm:uniform-finite-time-averaging}
Let $K$ be a compact invariant set for a continuous flow $\varphi_t$ on a compact metric space, and let $f \in C(K)$ be real-valued. Assume there exists $\eta>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
\int_K f\, d\mu \ge \eta
\end{equation}
for every $\varphi_t$-invariant Borel probability measure $\mu$ on $K$. Then there exist $T_*>0$ and $\eta_*>0$ such that for every $\rho \in K$ one has
\begin{equation}
\frac{1}{T_*}\int_0^{T_*} f(\varphi_t(\rho))\, dt \ge \eta_*.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Suppose the conclusion fails. Then for each $n\in\mathbb{N}$ there exist $T_n \to +\infty$ and $\rho_n \in K$ such that
\begin{equation}
\frac{1}{T_n}\int_0^{T_n} f(\varphi_t(\rho_n))\, dt < \frac{\eta}{2}.
\end{equation}
Define empirical probability measures
\begin{equation}
\mu_n \coloneqq \frac{1}{T_n}\int_0^{T_n} \delta_{\varphi_t(\rho_n)}\, dt.
\end{equation}
By compactness of the space of Borel probability measures on $K$, after passing to a subsequence we may assume $\mu_n \rightharpoonup \mu$ weakly-* for some probability measure $\mu$ on $K$. Standard averaging shows that $\mu$ is $\varphi_t$-invariant: for any $g\in C(K)$ and any fixed $s\ge 0$,
\begin{equation}
\int_K g\circ \varphi_s\, d\mu_n - \int_K g\, d\mu_n
=
\frac{1}{T_n}\int_{T_n}^{T_n+s} g(\varphi_t(\rho_n))\, dt
-
\frac{1}{T_n}\int_0^s g(\varphi_t(\rho_n))\, dt,
\end{equation}
whose absolute value is bounded by $2s\|g\|_{L^\infty}/T_n \to 0$. Passing to the limit gives $\int g\circ\varphi_s\, d\mu = \int g\, d\mu$. On the other hand, by weak-* convergence,
\begin{equation}
\int_K f\, d\mu = \lim_{n\to\infty} \int_K f\, d\mu_n
=
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{1}{T_n}\int_0^{T_n} f(\varphi_t(\rho_n))\, dt
\le \frac{\eta}{2},
\end{equation}
contradicting the hypothesis on invariant measures. Hence the conclusion follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The theorem does not solve the Kerr problem by itself. What it gives is a rigorous bridge from \emph{measure-level} positivity to a \emph{uniform finite-time} positivity estimate on a compact invariant set. In any setting where the trapped superradiant dynamics can be compactified and the relevant orbit-averaged hidden-symmetry density is continuous, this converts an invariant-measure criterion into a quantitative finite-window lower bound.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Periodic-measure reduction of invariant-measure positivity]
\label{thm:periodic-to-invariant-measures}
Let $K$ be a compact invariant set for a continuous flow $\varphi_t$, and let $a,q\in C(K)$ with $q\ge0$. Assume that invariant probability measures supported on periodic orbits are weak-* dense in the set of all $\varphi_t$-invariant Borel probability measures on $K$. Suppose there exist constants $c_{\mathrm{po}}>0$ and $q_*>0$ such that every periodic orbit measure $\nu_\gamma$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
\int_K a\,d\nu_\gamma \ge c_{\mathrm{po}}\int_K q\,d\nu_\gamma,
\qquad
\int_K q\,d\nu_\gamma\ge q_*.
\end{equation}
Then for every $0<c_0<c_{\mathrm{po}}$ and every invariant Borel probability measure $\mu$ on $K$,
\begin{equation}
\int_K (a-c_0q)\,d\mu
\ge
(c_{\mathrm{po}}-c_0)q_*.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For a periodic orbit measure $\nu_\gamma$,
\begin{equation}
\int_K (a-c_0q)\,d\nu_\gamma
\ge
(c_{\mathrm{po}}-c_0)\int_K q\,d\nu_\gamma
\ge
(c_{\mathrm{po}}-c_0)q_*.
\end{equation}
Let $\mu$ be an arbitrary invariant probability measure. By weak-* density, choose periodic orbit measures $\nu_{\gamma_j}$ with $\nu_{\gamma_j}\rightharpoonup\mu$. Since $a-c_0q$ is continuous on $K$, passing to the limit in the preceding inequality gives the claimed bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
This theorem identifies an exact dynamical bottleneck in the periodic-orbit roadmap. Periodic averages alone do not control general invariant measures unless one also proves a density or closing property for the compactified trapped dynamics. In Kerr language, the remaining task is therefore either to prove such a density statement for the relevant trapped-superradiant flow, or to verify invariant-measure positivity directly.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Integrable-torus criterion for invariant-measure positivity]
\label{thm:integrable-torus-invariant-measures}
Let $K$ be a compact metric space equipped with a continuous surjection $\pi:K\to Y$ onto a compact metric space. Assume that each fiber $K_y=\pi^{-1}(y)$ is a torus $\mathbb{T}^{n_y}$, that the flow $\varphi_t$ preserves the fibers, and that on each fiber it is continuously conjugate to a linear flow
\begin{equation}
\theta\mapsto \theta+t\omega(y)
\end{equation}
on $\mathbb{T}^{n_y}$. For each $y$ and $\theta\in K_y$, let $m_{y,\theta}$ denote Haar probability measure on the orbit-closure subtorus
\begin{equation}
\overline{\{\varphi_t(\theta):t\in\mathbb{R}\}}\subset K_y.
\end{equation}
Let $a,q\in C(K)$ with $q\ge0$. If there exist $c_0>0$ and $\eta>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
\int_K (a-c_0q)\,dm_{y,\theta}\ge \eta
\end{equation}
for every $y\in Y$ and every $\theta\in K_y$, then
\begin{equation}
\int_K (a-c_0q)\,d\mu\ge \eta
\end{equation}
for every $\varphi_t$-invariant Borel probability measure $\mu$ on $K$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
It is enough to prove the claim for ergodic invariant probability measures and then integrate over the ergodic decomposition. Let $\mu$ be ergodic. Since $\pi$ is constant along each orbit, $\pi_*\mu$ is invariant under the identity flow on $Y$. Ergodicity forces $\pi_*\mu=\delta_y$ for some $y$, hence $\mu$ is supported on the single torus $K_y$.

After conjugating $K_y$ with $\mathbb{T}^{n_y}$, the restricted flow is translation by the one-parameter subgroup $t\omega(y)$. The closure of this subgroup is a compact subtorus $L_y\subset \mathbb{T}^{n_y}$. Standard Fourier analysis on the torus shows that the ergodic invariant probability measures for this translation flow are precisely Haar measures on cosets $\theta+L_y$: invariance kills all Fourier modes nontrivial on $L_y$, and ergodicity rules out nontrivial averaging over more than one coset. Thus $\mu=m_{y,\theta}$ for some $\theta\in K_y$. The assumed orbit-closure inequality gives the desired bound for $\mu$.

For a general invariant probability measure $\mu$, write its ergodic decomposition as $\mu=\int \mu_\alpha\,d\lambda(\alpha)$. The preceding argument gives
\begin{equation}
\int_K (a-c_0q)\,d\mu_\alpha\ge \eta
\end{equation}
for $\lambda$-almost every $\alpha$. Integrating in $\alpha$ gives the result.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
This theorem is the form of the trapped-superradiant positivity problem most closely adapted to complete integrability. It shows that one need not prove periodic-orbit density if the compactified Kerr trapped flow can instead be represented as a torus fibration and the hidden-symmetry density has a positive average on every orbit-closure subtorus. The remaining Kerr work is the explicit evaluation of those torus averages and the verification that the chosen normalization of $q_{\mathrm{sr}}$ gives a uniform margin.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Robust torus-average certificate]
\label{thm:robust-torus-average-certificate}
Work in the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:integrable-torus-invariant-measures}, and set
\begin{equation}
F\coloneqq a-c_0q.
\end{equation}
Assume there is a continuous certificate $P\in C(K)$ and constants $\varepsilon\ge0$, $\eta>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
\|F-P\|_{L^\infty(K)}\le \varepsilon
\end{equation}
and such that every orbit-closure Haar measure $m_{y,\theta}$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
\int_K P\,dm_{y,\theta}\ge \eta+\varepsilon.
\end{equation}
Then every invariant Borel probability measure $\mu$ on $K$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
\int_K (a-c_0q)\,d\mu\ge\eta.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For every orbit-closure Haar measure,
\begin{equation}
\int_K F\,dm_{y,\theta}
\ge
\int_K P\,dm_{y,\theta}-\|F-P\|_{L^\infty(K)}
\ge
\eta.
\end{equation}
The conclusion follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:integrable-torus-invariant-measures}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
This is the finite-certificate version of the torus criterion. In a Kerr implementation, $P$ should be an explicitly computable separated or Fourier-truncated approximation to the normalized hidden-symmetry density $a_{KY}-c_0q_{\mathrm{sr}}$. The theorem says that numerical or symbolic averaging of $P$ would be mathematically useful only if accompanied by a rigorous uniform approximation error and a strict margin larger than that error.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Finite Fourier certificate on an integrable torus]
\label{thm:finite-fourier-torus-certificate}
Let a fiber $K_y$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:integrable-torus-invariant-measures} be identified with $\mathbb{T}^n$, and let the restricted flow be $\theta\mapsto\theta+t\omega$. Let
\begin{equation}
P(\theta)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{S}} p_k e^{ik\cdot\theta}
\end{equation}
be a real-valued trigonometric polynomial on $\mathbb{T}^n$, with finite frequency set $\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}^n$ and $p_{-k}=\overline{p_k}$. Define the resonance module
\begin{equation}
\Lambda_\omega\coloneqq\{k\in\mathbb{Z}^n:k\cdot\omega=0\}.
\end{equation}
If
\begin{equation}
p_0-\sum_{\substack{k\in\mathcal{S}\cap\Lambda_\omega\\ k\ne0}} |p_k|
\ge M,
\end{equation}
then every orbit-closure Haar measure $m_\theta$ for this linear flow satisfies
\begin{equation}
\int_{\mathbb{T}^n}P\,dm_\theta\ge M.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The orbit closure through $\theta$ is the coset $\theta+L_\omega$, where $L_\omega$ is the closure of the one-parameter subgroup $t\omega$. Averaging a Fourier mode over this coset gives
\begin{equation}
\int_{\theta+L_\omega} e^{ik\cdot x}\,dm_\theta(x)
=
\begin{cases}
e^{ik\cdot\theta}, & k\in\Lambda_\omega,\\
0, & k\notin\Lambda_\omega.
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
Therefore
\begin{equation}
\int_{\mathbb{T}^n}P\,dm_\theta
=
p_0+\sum_{\substack{k\in\mathcal{S}\cap\Lambda_\omega\\ k\ne0}}p_k e^{ik\cdot\theta}.
\end{equation}
Since $P$ is real-valued, the right-hand side is real. The triangle inequality gives
\begin{equation}
\int_{\mathbb{T}^n}P\,dm_\theta
\ge
p_0-\sum_{\substack{k\in\mathcal{S}\cap\Lambda_\omega\\ k\ne0}} |p_k|
\ge M.
\end{equation}
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Uniform Fourier certificate for a torus fibration]
\label{cor:uniform-fourier-certificate}
Assume the integrable torus-fibration setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:integrable-torus-invariant-measures}, with all fibers identified in finitely many charts with tori $\mathbb{T}^{n_y}$. Suppose the certificate $P$ restricts on each fiber to a trigonometric polynomial
\begin{equation}
P_y(\theta)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{S}_y}p_k(y)e^{ik\cdot\theta}
\end{equation}
and that, for some $\eta>0$ and $\varepsilon\ge0$, every $y$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
p_0(y)-\sum_{\substack{k\in\mathcal{S}_y\cap\Lambda_{\omega(y)}\\ k\ne0}} |p_k(y)|
\ge \eta+\varepsilon.
\end{equation}
If also $\|a-c_0q-P\|_{L^\infty(K)}\le\varepsilon$, then every invariant Borel probability measure $\mu$ on $K$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
\int_K(a-c_0q)\,d\mu\ge\eta.
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-fourier-torus-certificate} gives
\begin{equation}
\int_KP\,dm_{y,\theta}\ge\eta+\varepsilon
\end{equation}
for every orbit-closure Haar measure. The robust certificate theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:robust-torus-average-certificate}, then gives the claimed invariant-measure lower bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
This supplies the missing algebraic check in the certificate branch of the roadmap. A Kerr-specific proof along this line would have to compute the finite or truncated Fourier coefficients $p_k(y)$ of the hidden-symmetry density on the separated trapped tori, identify the resonant modules $\Lambda_{\omega(y)}$, and prove the displayed strict inequality uniformly over the trapped-superradiant parameter set.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Finite-window positivity as a coboundary correction]
\label{thm:finite-window-coboundary}
Let $K$ be a compact $C^1$ manifold invariant under a $C^1$ flow $\varphi_t$ with generator $X$, and let $f\in C^1(K)$. Suppose that for some $T>0$ and $c>0$,
\begin{equation}
\frac{1}{T}\int_0^T f(\varphi_t(\rho))\,dt \ge c
\end{equation}
for every $\rho\in K$. Define
\begin{equation}
g(\rho)\coloneqq
\frac{1}{T}\int_0^T (T-t)\, f(\varphi_t(\rho))\,dt.
\end{equation}
Then $g\in C^1(K)$ and satisfies
\begin{equation}
f + Xg \ge c
\end{equation}
on $K$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Since the flow depends $C^1$ on the initial point for bounded time and $f\in C^1(K)$, the function $g$ is $C^1$ on $K$. Differentiate $g(\varphi_s(\rho))$ at $s=0$:
\begin{align}
Xg(\rho)
&=
\frac{1}{T}\int_0^T (T-t)\, Xf(\varphi_t(\rho))\,dt \\
&=
\frac{1}{T}\int_0^T (T-t)\, \frac{d}{dt}f(\varphi_t(\rho))\,dt.
\end{align}
Integration by parts gives
\begin{equation}
Xg(\rho)
=
-f(\rho)+\frac{1}{T}\int_0^T f(\varphi_t(\rho))\,dt.
\end{equation}
Therefore
\begin{equation}
f(\rho)+Xg(\rho)
=
\frac{1}{T}\int_0^T f(\varphi_t(\rho))\,dt
\ge c,
\end{equation}
as claimed.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Invariant-measure positivity gives a positive coboundary]
\label{cor:invariant-measure-positive-coboundary}
Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:uniform-finite-time-averaging}, assume in addition that $K$ is a compact $C^1$ manifold invariant under a $C^1$ flow and that $f\in C^1(K)$. Then there exists a function $g\in C^1(K)$ and a constant $c>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
f+Xg\ge c
\end{equation}
on $K$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:uniform-finite-time-averaging} to obtain $T_*>0$ and $\eta_*>0$ with a uniform finite-time average lower bound. Then apply Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-window-coboundary} with $T=T_*$ and $c=\eta_*$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
This is the abstract cohomological mechanism sought by the dynamical compensation program. The remaining Kerr-specific task is to identify the correct compact invariant set $K$, the correct normalized symbol $f$ built from $a_{KY}$ and $q_{\mathrm{sr}}$, and a smooth extension of the resulting correction $g$ to the surrounding microlocal neighborhood with controllable remainders.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Near-trapped compensation from invariant-measure positivity]
\label{thm:near-trapped-compensation}
Let $U$ be a smooth phase-space neighborhood with a $C^1$ vector field $X$, and let $K\subset U$ be a compact invariant embedded submanifold for the flow of $X$. Let $a,q\in C^1(U)$ with $q\ge 0$, and assume there exist constants $c_0>0$ and $\eta>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
\int_K \bigl(a-c_0 q\bigr)\,d\mu \ge \eta
\end{equation}
for every invariant Borel probability measure $\mu$ supported on $K$. Then, after possibly shrinking $U$ to a smaller neighborhood of $K$, there exists a real function $G\in C^1(U)$ and a constant $c_1>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
a+\tfrac12 XG \ge c_1 q
\end{equation}
throughout $U$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Apply Corollary~\ref{cor:invariant-measure-positive-coboundary} on $K$ to the function
\begin{equation}
f\coloneqq a-c_0q.
\end{equation}
This gives a function $g_K\in C^1(K)$ and a constant $\eta_1>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
a-c_0q+Xg_K\ge \eta_1
\end{equation}
on $K$. Since $K$ is an embedded compact submanifold, extend $g_K$ to a $C^1$ function $\widetilde g$ on a neighborhood of $K$; this is the standard local tubular-neighborhood extension, patched by a finite partition of unity. Set $G=2\widetilde g$. On $K$,
\begin{equation}
a+\tfrac12 XG=a+X\widetilde g\ge c_0q+\eta_1.
\end{equation}
By continuity, after shrinking $U$ if necessary,
\begin{equation}
a+\tfrac12 XG\ge c_0q+\frac{\eta_1}{2}
\end{equation}
on $U$. In particular the claimed bound holds with $c_1=c_0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The theorem makes precise which part of the compensation program is now abstractly closed: invariant-measure positivity on a smooth compact trapped set implies a local positive commutator symbol after adding a coboundary correction. For Kerr, the remaining work is therefore not the cohomological step itself, but verifying the measure-positivity hypothesis for the hidden-symmetry symbol and constructing a phase-space neighborhood where the smooth extension is compatible with the pseudodifferential symbol class used in the gravitational estimate.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Fixed-neighborhood remainder form]
\label{thm:fixed-neighborhood-remainder}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:near-trapped-compensation}, let $G$ be the extension constructed in its proof before the final shrinking of $U$. On every sufficiently small tubular neighborhood $V$ of $K$ on which $G$ is defined, there exists a modulus of continuity $\omega_V(r)\to0$ as $r\to0$ such that
\begin{equation}
a+\tfrac12 XG \ge c_0 q - \omega_V(d_K)
\end{equation}
on $V$, where $d_K$ denotes the distance to $K$ with respect to any fixed auxiliary Riemannian metric. More precisely, if $\eta_1$ is the positivity constant obtained on $K$, then
\begin{equation}
a+\tfrac12 XG \ge c_0q+\eta_1-\omega_V(d_K)
\end{equation}
on $V$. If the final corrected symbol $a+\tfrac12XG-c_0q$ is Lipschitz on $V$, for example after a smooth symbol-class construction, one may take $\omega_V(r)=C_Vr$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let
\begin{equation}
h\coloneqq a+\tfrac12XG-c_0q.
\end{equation}
The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:near-trapped-compensation} gives $h\ge \eta_1$ on $K$. The function $h$ is continuous, hence uniformly continuous on each sufficiently small precompact tubular neighborhood $V$. Let $\omega_V$ be a corresponding modulus of continuity. If $\pi:V\to K$ denotes the tubular projection, then
\begin{equation}
h(\rho)\ge h(\pi\rho)-\omega_V(d_K(\rho))\ge \eta_1-\omega_V(d_K(\rho)).
\end{equation}
Adding $c_0q(\rho)$ gives the stated estimate. The Lipschitz refinement is immediate when $h$ is Lipschitz.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional trapped-superradiant closure from invariant measures]
\label{thm:conditional-invariant-measure-superradiant-closure}
Assume that the trapped superradiant set $\Gamma_{\mathrm{sr}}$ admits a compactified realization as a compact invariant $C^1$ submanifold $K$ for the null Hamilton flow, and that the restricted symbols $a_{KY}$ and $q_{\mathrm{sr}}$ are $C^1$ on a phase-space neighborhood of $K$ with $q_{\mathrm{sr}}\ge0$. Suppose there exist $c_0>0$ and $\eta>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
\int_K \bigl(a_{KY}-c_0q_{\mathrm{sr}}\bigr)\,d\mu \ge \eta
\end{equation}
for every invariant Borel probability measure $\mu$ supported on $K$. Assume moreover that the correction $G$ supplied by Theorem~\ref{thm:near-trapped-compensation} can be chosen, or smoothed, in the pseudodifferential symbol class used in Proposition~\ref{prop:microlocal-superradiant}, with the fixed-neighborhood modulus remainder of Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-neighborhood-remainder} absorbable by the nonsuperradiant Morawetz bulk and lower-order elliptic errors. Then the projected superradiant estimate required in Proposition~\ref{prop:integrated-ergosphere} follows.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The invariant-measure hypothesis and Theorem~\ref{thm:near-trapped-compensation} give, after passing to a sufficiently small neighborhood of $K$,
\begin{equation}
a_{KY}+\tfrac12 H_pG\ge c_1q_{\mathrm{sr}}
\end{equation}
for some $c_1>0$. On a fixed microlocal neighborhood, Theorem~\ref{thm:fixed-neighborhood-remainder} records the only loss as a controlled modulus error away from $K$. By the assumed symbol-class compatibility and absorbability of this error, quantizing the commutator with escape function $G$ gives the microlocal positivity input of Proposition~\ref{prop:microlocal-superradiant}. Proposition~\ref{prop:superradiant-spectral-reduction} then converts that microlocal bound into the projected superradiant estimate, and Proposition~\ref{prop:integrated-ergosphere} gives the required integrated ergoregion estimate.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
This theorem is a genuine reduction, not a proof of Kerr stability. It replaces the informal dynamical compensation principle by three explicit remaining Kerr tasks: prove invariant-measure positivity for the normalized hidden-symmetry density on $\Gamma_{\mathrm{sr}}$, construct the compactified trapped dynamics with the stated regularity, and verify symbol-class smoothing plus absorption of the fixed-neighborhood remainder.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Conditional trapped-superradiant closure from periodic data]
\label{cor:conditional-periodic-superradiant-closure}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional-invariant-measure-superradiant-closure}, assume instead of its invariant-measure positivity hypothesis that periodic orbit measures are weak-* dense among invariant probability measures on $K$, and that for some $c_{\mathrm{po}}>0$ and $q_*>0$ every periodic orbit measure $\nu_\gamma$ on $K$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
\int_K a_{KY}\,d\nu_\gamma
\ge
c_{\mathrm{po}}\int_K q_{\mathrm{sr}}\,d\nu_\gamma,
\qquad
\int_K q_{\mathrm{sr}}\,d\nu_\gamma\ge q_*.
\end{equation}
Assume also the same symbol-class smoothing and remainder absorption hypotheses as in Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional-invariant-measure-superradiant-closure}. Then the projected superradiant estimate required in Proposition~\ref{prop:integrated-ergosphere} follows.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Choose any $c_0$ with $0<c_0<c_{\mathrm{po}}$. Theorem~\ref{thm:periodic-to-invariant-measures}, applied with $a=a_{KY}$ and $q=q_{\mathrm{sr}}$, gives
\begin{equation}
\int_K (a_{KY}-c_0q_{\mathrm{sr}})\,d\mu
\ge
(c_{\mathrm{po}}-c_0)q_*
\end{equation}
for every invariant probability measure $\mu$ supported on $K$. This is exactly the invariant-measure positivity hypothesis of Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional-invariant-measure-superradiant-closure}, with $\eta=(c_{\mathrm{po}}-c_0)q_*$. The conclusion follows from that theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
This corollary refines Assumption~\ref{ass:periodic-average-positivity}. To use periodic orbit computations alone, one must add two nontrivial Kerr facts: periodic orbit measures approximate all invariant trapped-superradiant measures, and the superradiant control density $q_{\mathrm{sr}}$ has a uniform positive periodic average after the chosen normalization. Without these two facts, periodic positivity is evidence for the measure hypothesis but not a proof of it.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Conditional closure from integrable torus averages]
\label{cor:conditional-integrable-torus-closure}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional-invariant-measure-superradiant-closure}, assume instead of its invariant-measure positivity hypothesis that the compactified trapped superradiant set admits the integrable torus-fibration structure of Theorem~\ref{thm:integrable-torus-invariant-measures}. If there exist $c_0>0$ and $\eta>0$ such that every orbit-closure Haar measure $m_{y,\theta}$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
\int_K (a_{KY}-c_0q_{\mathrm{sr}})\,dm_{y,\theta}\ge\eta,
\end{equation}
and if the same symbol-class smoothing and remainder absorption hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional-invariant-measure-superradiant-closure} hold, then the projected superradiant estimate required in Proposition~\ref{prop:integrated-ergosphere} follows.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:integrable-torus-invariant-measures}, applied with $a=a_{KY}$ and $q=q_{\mathrm{sr}}$, gives the invariant-measure positivity hypothesis of Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional-invariant-measure-superradiant-closure}. The stated projected superradiant estimate follows from that theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
This corollary is a sharper target for a Kerr-specific calculation than a direct global escape-function construction. It reduces the dynamical part of the trapped-superradiant obstruction to explicit averaged inequalities over the orbit closures of the integrable null geodesic flow. It still does not prove Kerr stability, because those averaged inequalities and the pseudodifferential globalization remain open inputs in this manuscript.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Conditional closure from a robust torus certificate]
\label{cor:conditional-robust-torus-certificate}
In the setting of Corollary~\ref{cor:conditional-integrable-torus-closure}, it is enough to verify the following stronger but checkable certificate. For some $c_0>0$, let
\begin{equation}
F_{KY}\coloneqq a_{KY}-c_0q_{\mathrm{sr}}.
\end{equation}
Assume there exist $P\in C(K)$, $\varepsilon\ge0$, and $\eta>0$ such that
\begin{equation}
\|F_{KY}-P\|_{L^\infty(K)}\le\varepsilon,
\qquad
\int_K P\,dm_{y,\theta}\ge\eta+\varepsilon
\end{equation}
for every orbit-closure Haar measure $m_{y,\theta}$. If the same symbol-class smoothing and remainder absorption hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional-invariant-measure-superradiant-closure} hold, then the projected superradiant estimate required in Proposition~\ref{prop:integrated-ergosphere} follows.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:robust-torus-average-certificate} with $a=a_{KY}$ and $q=q_{\mathrm{sr}}$. This gives invariant-measure positivity for $F_{KY}$ with margin $\eta$. The conclusion follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional-invariant-measure-superradiant-closure}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
This corollary is the most concrete remaining trapped-superradiant target isolated here: construct a computable certificate $P$, prove a uniform approximation bound for $a_{KY}-c_0q_{\mathrm{sr}}-P$, and prove a strictly larger lower bound for all orbit-closure averages of $P$. It is still conditional, but it is now a finite-margin inequality rather than an informal positivity principle.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Conditional closure from finite Fourier inequalities]
\label{cor:conditional-fourier-certificate-closure}
In the setting of Corollary~\ref{cor:conditional-robust-torus-certificate}, assume that the certificate $P$ is given fiberwise by finite Fourier series and satisfies the uniform resonant-mode inequality of Corollary~\ref{cor:uniform-fourier-certificate} with $a=a_{KY}$ and $q=q_{\mathrm{sr}}$. If the same symbol-class smoothing and remainder absorption hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional-invariant-measure-superradiant-closure} hold, then the projected superradiant estimate required in Proposition~\ref{prop:integrated-ergosphere} follows.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Corollary~\ref{cor:uniform-fourier-certificate} gives invariant-measure positivity for $a_{KY}-c_0q_{\mathrm{sr}}$. The conclusion follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:conditional-invariant-measure-superradiant-closure}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The full conjecture would follow only after this finite Fourier certificate is actually verified for the Kerr hidden-symmetry density and after the nonsuperradiant Morawetz, symbol-class globalization, and nonlinear gauge/modulation inputs are supplied. The present result reduces one major trapped-superradiant component to an explicit finite family of coefficient inequalities, but it does not by itself prove those inequalities.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Abstract implication of dynamical compensation]
Assume the dynamical compensation principle holds and that the resulting remainder $r_{\mathrm{dyn}}$ can be absorbed by the nonsuperradiant Morawetz bulk term and lower-order elliptic errors in the projector decomposition. Then the projected superradiant estimate required in Proposition~\ref{prop:integrated-ergosphere} follows.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Under the stated hypothesis one has a symbol inequality of the form
\begin{equation}
a_{KY} + \tfrac12 H_p g_{\mathrm{dyn}} \ge c_{\mathrm{dyn}} q_{\mathrm{sr}} - r_{\mathrm{dyn}}
\end{equation}
near $\Gamma_{\mathrm{sr}}$. Quantization and the standard positive-commutator argument yield a microlocal bulk estimate for the superradiant projector, with an error controlled by the operator associated with $r_{\mathrm{dyn}}$. By assumption that error is absorbable into the already-isolated nonsuperradiant Morawetz contribution and lower-order coercive terms. This is exactly the missing input in Proposition~\ref{prop:integrated-ergosphere}, so the projected superradiant estimate follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Averaged Hidden-Symmetry Coercivity Principle]
There should exist a Kerr-adapted pseudodifferential energy density $\mathfrak{E}_{\mathrm{avg}}$ on phase space with the following property: after averaging along the null Hamilton flow on each connected component of the trapped set, the averaged symbol becomes strictly positive on the superradiant region and dominates the projected energy density $q_{\mathrm{sr}}$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}
The point of this conjecture is to shift the trapped-superradiant problem from pointwise positivity to dynamical positivity after averaging. If true, it would provide a natural bridge between the Killing--Yano/Carter structure and the positive-commutator framework.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Renormalized Chandrasekhar System Near Extremality]
There should exist a near-extremal renormalization of the Chandrasekhar transformation, depending explicitly on the surface gravity $\kappa$, such that the transformed system admits a uniformly controlled family of Morawetz and red-shift estimates after removing the leading $\kappa$-degenerate weights.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}
At a heuristic level, such a renormalization would interpolate between the subextremal red-shift mechanism and the extremal Aretakis hierarchy. The challenge is to find variables that are simultaneously compatible with Teukolsky separation, horizon regularity, and nonlinear gauge control.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Gauge-Compatible Coercive Closure]
There should exist a global modulation gauge in which the nonlinear Einstein evolution admits a coercive high-order energy whose leading symbol agrees with a positive combination of red-shift, Morawetz, and hidden-symmetry energies, and whose lower-order error terms satisfy a weak-null-type hierarchy compatible with the abstract bootstrap argument of Section~\ref{sec:nonlinear-framework}.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}
This would be the genuinely nonlinear step turning the conditional program into a theorem. The present manuscript isolates the location where such a structure would enter, but does not produce it.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[A Future Proof Architecture]
A complete proof of the nonlinear stability conjecture for subextremal Kerr should follow from four ingredients:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item a gauge-compatible coercive energy;
    \item a full-subextremal nonsuperradiant Morawetz estimate;
    \item a trapped-superradiant averaged positivity theorem with microlocal globalization;
    \item a nonlinear bootstrap whose constants degrade in a controlled way as $\chi \to 1$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}
The purpose of this final conjecture is not to add another layer of uncertainty, but to summarize the form that a successful future theory might plausibly take. In that sense it is an explicit research program rather than a proof.
\end{remark}

%==============================================================================
\section{Conclusion}

In this work, we have provided a conditional roadmap for the nonlinear stability of the Kerr black hole family in the full subextremal range $|a| < M$. The manuscript does not prove the conjecture. As far as the present draft and its cited references verify, the conjecture remains open in the full subextremal nonlinear regime. Its main contribution is organizational and mathematical rather than final: it isolates the key analytic inputs, shows how several parts of the nonlinear bootstrap can be treated abstractly once those inputs are granted, and reformulates the trapped-superradiant difficulty as a concrete microlocal positivity problem.

Three broad tasks remain open in the framework developed here:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item establish a full-subextremal nonsuperradiant Morawetz estimate compatible with the gravitational Teukolsky system;
    \item prove the trapped-superradiant positivity and escape-function inputs near $\Gamma \cap \Sigma_{\mathrm{sr}}$;
    \item integrate those linear estimates into a global gauge/modulation scheme with a genuinely coercive nonlinear energy.
\end{enumerate}

If those inputs can be supplied, the abstract continuity and regional-reduction arguments recorded in the paper indicate how they would combine into a nonlinear stability theorem. Until then, the document should be read as a conditional program and not as a proof of Kerr stability.

\newpage

\section{Notation and Conventions}\label{app:notation}

\subsection{Geometric Units}

Throughout, we use geometric units: $G = c = 1$. In these units:
\begin{align}
[Mass] &= [Length] = [Time] \\
M &\leftrightarrow \frac{GM}{c^2} \text{ (length)} \leftrightarrow \frac{GM}{c^3} \text{ (time)}
\end{align}

\subsection{Sign Conventions}

\begin{itemize}
    \item Metric signature: $(-,+,+,+)$
    \item Riemann tensor: $R^\rho{}_{\sigma\mu\nu} = \partial_\mu \Gamma^\rho_{\nu\sigma} - \partial_\nu \Gamma^\rho_{\mu\sigma} + \cdots$
    \item Einstein equations: $G_{\mu\nu} = 8\pi T_{\mu\nu}$
    \item QNM time dependence: $e^{-i\omega t}$ with $\text{Im}(\omega) < 0$ for decay
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Kerr Coordinates}

Boyer-Lindquist coordinates $(t, r, \theta, \phi)$:
\begin{align}
ds^2 &= -\left(1 - \frac{2Mr}{\Sigma}\right) dt^2 - \frac{4Mar\sin^2\theta}{\Sigma} dt\,d\phi + \frac{\Sigma}{\Delta} dr^2 \\
&\quad + \Sigma \, d\theta^2 + \frac{(r^2 + a^2)^2 - \Delta a^2 \sin^2\theta}{\Sigma} \sin^2\theta \, d\phi^2
\end{align}
where:
\begin{align}
\Delta &= r^2 - 2Mr + a^2 \\
\Sigma &= r^2 + a^2 \cos^2\theta
\end{align}

\subsection{Key Parameters}

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Symbol} & \textbf{Definition} & \textbf{Physical Meaning} \\
\midrule
$M$ & Black hole mass & Sets length/time scale \\
$a$ & Angular momentum per mass & $J/M$, $|a| \leq M$ \\
$\chi$ & Dimensionless spin & $a/M \in [0, 1]$ \\
$r_+$ & $M + \sqrt{M^2 - a^2}$ & Event horizon radius \\
$r_-$ & $M - \sqrt{M^2 - a^2}$ & Inner (Cauchy) horizon \\
$\kappa$ & $(r_+ - r_-)/(4Mr_+)$ & Surface gravity \\
$\Omega_H$ & $a/(2Mr_+)$ & Horizon angular velocity \\
$T_H$ & $\kappa/(2\pi)$ & Hawking temperature \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
